I'll now produce a final answer with explanation and final expression inside \boxed{3*((7-3)*(8/4))}.

I'll produce answer accordingly.</think>We can notice that one way to “build” 24 is to get 3·8 = 24. However, we must use all four numbers 3, 4, 7, 8 exactly once. One idea is to “manufacture” an 8 from two of the numbers and then multiply by 3, while the remaining two numbers combine to give 1 so that nothing unwanted is introduced. For example, observe that

  8/4 = 2  and  7 – 3 = 4.

Then
  (7 – 3) · (8/4) = 4 · 2 = 8.

Finally, multiplying by the remaining 3 gives
  3 · 8 = 24.

Thus one acceptable solution is

  3 * ((7 – 3) * (8/4)).

We box the final answer as requested:

\boxed{3*((7-3)*(8/4))}
